D:\App\Ravum-flow\crates\flow\src\commands.rs:
    1|       |//! Flow CLI commands: init, doctor, status, run, publish, reconcile, explain,
    2|       |//! disable/enable. Everything returns structured JSON; refusals are explicit.
    3|       |//!
    4|       |//! The publication gate is the whole point of this crate, so the rules are
    5|       |//! stated once, here, and enforced in one place:
    6|       |//!
    7|       |//! * a receipt proves exactly one *effective policy* on exactly one Git tree;
    8|       |//! * a selective (`--obligation`) run is diagnostic and never publishable;
    9|       |//! * `publish` recomputes the policy identity instead of trusting the receipt;
   10|       |//! * recovery observes, never guesses, and refuses to report a corrupt journal
   11|       |//!   as a success.
   12|       |
   13|       |use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};
   14|       |use std::fs;
   15|       |use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
   16|       |
   17|       |use crate::gitcli::{self, Git, Obligation};
   18|       |use crate::json::{self, Json};
   19|       |use crate::sha256;
   20|       |use crate::state::{self, Config, Receipt};
   21|       |
   22|      1|fn refused(why: impl Into<String>) -> Json {
   23|      1|    Json::obj(vec![
   24|      1|        ("status", Json::str("REFUSED")),
   25|      1|        ("reason", Json::str(why.into())),
   26|       |    ])
   27|      1|}
   28|       |
   29|       |/// A refusal that carries a stable machine-readable code. The `status` stays
   30|       |/// `REFUSED` so callers (and the exit-code contract) never have to special-case
   31|       |/// the reason, while tests and operators can assert on the precise cause.
   32|     69|fn refused_code(code: &str, why: impl Into<String>) -> Json {
   33|     69|    Json::obj(vec![
   34|     69|        ("status", Json::str("REFUSED")),
   35|     69|        ("code", Json::str(code)),
   36|     69|        ("reason", Json::str(why.into())),
   37|       |    ])
   38|     69|}
   39|       |
   40|    107|fn short(digest: &str) -> String {
   41|    107|    digest.chars().take(12).collect()
   42|    107|}

  935|       |/// (`.git/info/attributes`, `core.attributesFile`) — and any driver those
  936|       |/// attributes name). A filter can rewrite content **without changing its
  937|       |/// length**, so a size comparison is not a proof; and a "no conversion looks
  938|       |/// possible" guess is not one either, because the attribute sources include
  939|       |/// host-local files Flow cannot enumerate exhaustively. Every regular file is

    1|       |//! Git plumbing through the `git` executable (no libgit2, no dependency) and a
    2|       |//! bounded obligation runner with an explicit environment allowlist.
    3|       |//!
    4|       |//! Three independent hardenings live here:
    5|       |//!
    6|       |//! * every `git` invocation is built from a scrubbed environment, so a hostile
    7|       |//!   host (or a test harness) cannot redirect plumbing at another repository,
    8|       |//!   object store, index or config through `GIT_*` variables;
    9|       |//! * obligations run with a bounded capture (at most [`MAX_STREAM_BYTES`] kept
   10|       |//!   per stream) and a bounded wall clock, and their reader threads are never
   11|       |//!   joined, so a descendant that survives the kill and keeps a pipe open can
   12|       |//!   delay the call by at most [`READER_GRACE`], never indefinitely.
   13|       |
   14|       |use std::ffi::OsString;
   15|       |use std::io::{Read, Write};
   16|       |use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
   17|       |use std::process::{Child, Command, Stdio};

   47|       |    /// Run one Git plumbing command with bounded memory, bounded wall clock and
   48|       |    /// an optional explicit index.
   49|       |    ///
   50|       |    /// * `stdin` is written to the child and the pipe is then closed; it is
   51|       |    ///   written from a detached thread so a child that produces output before
   52|       |    ///   reading its input cannot deadlock the call.
   53|       |    /// * `index` sets `GIT_INDEX_FILE` **for this invocation only**. The ambient
   54|       |    ///   environment is still scrubbed, so an inherited `GIT_INDEX_FILE` is
   55|       |    ///   ignored; the path must be absolute, and the caller is responsible for
   56|       |    ///   having created it (this is how proposal assembly avoids the user's
   57|       |    ///   index).
   58|       |    /// * At most [`MAX_GIT_STREAM_BYTES`] is retained per stream. Past that the
   59|       |    ///   call **fails** with `GIT_OUTPUT_TOO_LARGE` instead of truncating: a
   60|       |    ///   clipped Git answer is not an answer, and it must never be parsed as one.
   61|       |    /// * The wall clock is bounded by [`GIT_TIMEOUT_S`]; the child's tree is
   62|       |    ///   killed and the call fails with `GIT_TIMEOUT`.

  487|       |/// Run one obligation with bounded output and bounded wall-clock containment.
  488|       |///
  489|       |/// Bounds:
  490|       |/// * at most [`MAX_STREAM_BYTES`] retained per stream (the rest is drained and
  491|       |///   discarded), flagged through [`Outcome::stdout_truncated`] /
  492|       |///   [`Outcome::stderr_truncated`];
  493|       |/// * one timeout in seconds, after which the child tree is killed;
  494|       |/// * after the kill, readers get at most [`READER_GRACE`] to observe EOF.
  495|       |///
  496|       |/// Non-guarantees (explicit, not sandboxing):
  497|       |/// * Windows kills the tree with `taskkill /T /F`; a descendant that has
  498|       |///   re-parented itself or otherwise left the tree survives.
  499|       |/// * Unix spawns the child as a process-group leader and signals the group with
  500|       |///   `SIGKILL`; a descendant that calls `setsid`/`setpgid` escapes the group
  501|       |///   and survives.
  502|       |/// * A surviving descendant that inherited stdout/stderr keeps its pipe open.
  503|       |///   The reader thread for that stream is detached after the grace period, so
  504|       |///   the call returns; the thread (and the pipe) lives until the survivor exits
  505|       |///   or the process exits.
  506|       |// Resolve a program name through PATH and PATHEXT on Windows.
  507|       |//
  508|       |// CreateProcess only appends .exe, so a tool installed as a .cmd or .bat shim
  509|       |// (which is how npm installs tsc, npx, gradle, ...) is not found by name. Flow
  510|       |// resolves it the way a shell would and only then spawns it. When nothing
  511|       |// matches, the caller keeps the declared name so the failure is reported as
  512|       |// UNSUPPORTED with the name the policy asked for.
  513|       |#[cfg(windows)]

  651|       |/// Kill the child and, where the platform supports it, its descendants.
  652|       |///
  653|       |/// Windows: `taskkill /T /F` walks the process tree.
  654|       |///
  655|       |/// POSIX: the child was spawned as a process-group leader (see
  656|       |/// [`run_obligation`]), so the shell's `kill` builtin is asked to signal
  657|       |/// `-<pid>`; the plain PID is signalled as a fallback. A descendant that leaves
  658|       |/// the process group is not reached — the limitation is documented, never
  659|       |/// hidden. (No `unsafe` FFI: the POSIX shell builtin is used instead of
  660|       |/// `libc::kill`, keeping the crate dependency-free.)

  663|      1|    {

  665|      1|            .args(["/PID", &pid.to_string(), "/T", "/F"])
  666|      1|            .stdout(Stdio::null())
  667|      1|            .stderr(Stdio::null())

  681|       |                &group,
  682|       |                &direct,
  683|       |            ])

  685|       |            .stdout(Stdio::null())

  691|       |/// Redact values of secret-looking environment variables and known token shapes
  692|       |/// BEFORE a log is written to disk.
  693|       |///
  694|       |/// Name matching is deliberately broad (`SECRET`, `TOKEN`, `PASSWORD`, `PASSWD`,
  695|       |/// `PRIVATE_KEY`, `API_KEY`, `ACCESS_KEY`, `CREDENTIAL`, `AUTH`): over-redacting a
  696|       |/// value that happens to equal a credential is harmless, while leaking one is not.
  697|       |/// Shape matching covers the tokens that are recognisable without their variable
  698|       |/// name (GitHub `ghp_`/`gho_`/`ghs_`/`github_pat_`, OpenAI `sk-`, AWS `AKIA`/`ASIA`
  699|       |/// access-key ids). A secret that is printed from a file, with no matching
  700|       |/// environment variable and no recognisable shape, is **not** covered — there is no
  701|       |/// sandbox and no secret broker (documented non-guarantee).

D:\App\Ravum-flow\crates\flow\src\lock.rs:
    1|       |//! The local publication lock — contract `flow-lock/2`.
    2|       |//!
    3|       |//! What this lock is: a mutual-exclusion device between `flow` processes of the
    4|       |//! same working copy, protecting the short mutations (`publish`, `reconcile`) so
    5|       |//! two writers cannot interleave journal appends or both CAS the same ref.
    6|       |//!
    7|       |//! What it is **not**, and must never be described as:
    8|       |//!
    9|       |//! * a distributed lease — it only protects one `\.flow` directory on one host;
   10|       |//! * a fence against a foreign `git` writer, which never takes it;
   11|       |//! * keyed — any local writer with the same rights can rewrite it.
   12|       |//!
   13|       |//! Attribution rules (the whole point of v2):
   14|       |//!
   15|       |//! * a lock whose owner is provably alive is **never** stolen, whatever its age;
   16|       |//! * a lock is stolen only when its owner is **provably gone** (the PID is dead
   17|       |//!   and the file still carries the same nonce that was observed);
   18|       |//! * when liveness cannot be established (no `tasklist`/`kill`, an unreadable or
   19|       |//!   unparseable file, an empty file left by a crash between create and write, a
   20|       |//!   `kill -0` that failed for any reason other than "no such process") the
   21|       |//!   answer is [`LockError::Unknown`] — a refusal that names the file and asks
   22|       |//!   for a human, never a guess. `EPERM` is not "gone": a group-writable checkout
   23|       |//!   can hold a live lock owned by another user.
   24|       |//!
   25|       |//! Age is reported, never used to justify a steal. PID reuse therefore wedges the
   26|       |//! lock until someone removes it by hand. That is deliberate: a wedge is loud and
   27|       |//! recoverable, a stolen live lock is a silent double publication.
   28|       |
   29|       |use std::fs::{self, OpenOptions};
   30|       |use std::io::Write;
   31|       |use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
   32|       |use std::time::{Duration, Instant};
   33|       |
   34|       |pub const LOCK_SCHEMA: &str = "flow-lock/2";
   35|       |
   36|       |const ACQUIRE_BUDGET: Duration = Duration::from_secs(3);
   37|       |const POLL_INTERVAL: Duration = Duration::from_millis(50);
   38|       |

  333|       |
  334|       |/// Ask `kill -0` and read its message.
  335|       |///
  336|       |/// The exit status alone cannot answer the question: it is non-zero both for a
  337|       |/// process that is gone (ESRCH) and for a live process owned by another user
  338|       |/// (EPERM). Liveness is only "provable" when the message says the process does
  339|       |/// not exist; anything else is unknown, and unknown is never stolen.
  340|       |#[cfg(not(windows))]
  341|       |fn kill_liveness(pid: u32) -> Option<bool> {
  342|       |    let out = std::process::Command::new("kill")
  343|       |        .args(["-0", &pid.to_string()])
  344|       |        .env("LC_ALL", "C")
  345|       |        .output()
  346|       |        .ok()?;
  347|       |    if out.status.success() {
  348|       |        return Some(true);
  349|       |    }
  350|       |    let message = String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr).to_ascii_lowercase();
  351|       |    if message.contains("no such process") || message.contains("does not exist") {
  352|       |        Some(false)
  353|       |    } else {
  354|       |        None
  355|       |    }
  356|       |}
  357|       |
  358|       |/// On Linux the kernel's own view comes first: `/proc/<pid>` exists exactly while
  359|       |/// the pid exists, and it cannot confuse "gone" with "not mine". Only when the
  360|       |/// entry is invisible (hidepid, another PID namespace) does the probe ask `kill`.
  361|       |#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
  362|       |fn pid_alive(pid: u32) -> Option<bool> {
  363|       |    if Path::new(&format!("/proc/{pid}")).exists() {
  364|       |        return Some(true);
  365|       |    }
  366|       |    kill_liveness(pid)
  367|       |}
  368|       |
  369|       |#[cfg(all(unix, not(target_os = "linux")))]
  370|       |fn pid_alive(pid: u32) -> Option<bool> {
  371|       |    kill_liveness(pid)
  372|       |}

D:\App\Ravum-flow\crates\flow\src\main.rs:
    1|       |//! Ravum Flow — verified-tree receipts and exact-tree assembly for Git.
    2|       |//!
    3|       |//! Design constraints encoded here:
    4|       |//! * no dependency, no daemon, no network, no LLM;
    5|       |//! * a receipt names the exact Git tree OID that was checked;
    6|       |//! * a publication is ref-CAS + a durable intent/outcome journal;
    7|       |//! * anything not executed is `UNSUPPORTED`, `UNKNOWN` or `NOT_RUN` — never `PASSED`.
    8|       |//!
    9|       |//! The CLI prints machine-readable JSON for every command and exits 0 only for a
   10|       |//! status that means "the requested thing happened" (`PASSED`, `PUBLISHED`,
   11|       |//! `PLAN`, `INITIALIZED`, `APPROVED`, `ADMITTED`, `ASSEMBLED`, `RECORD`,
   12|       |//! `RECONCILED`, `EXPLAIN`, `STATUS`, `DISABLED`, `ENABLED`). Every refusal,
   13|       |//! failure and unknown outcome exits 1. A usage error exits 2.
   14|       |
   15|       |mod commands;
   16|       |mod gitcli;
   17|       |mod json;
   18|       |mod lock;
   19|       |mod paths;
   20|       |mod proposal;
   21|       |mod scratch;
   22|       |mod sha256;
   23|       |mod state;
   24|       |
   25|       |use std::path::PathBuf;
   26|       |use std::process::ExitCode;
   27|       |
   28|       |use json::Json;
   29|       |
   30|       |const USAGE: &str = "\
   31|       |flow — verified-tree receipts and exact-tree assembly for Git repositories
   32|       |       (local, offline, no daemon, no LLM)
   33|       |
   34|       |USAGE:
   35|       |  flow <command> [options]
   36|       |
   37|       |SETUP:
   38|       |  init [--force] [--adopt-policy]  create .flow/; adopt a repository policy only explicitly
   39|       |  approve                           record local approval of the current .flow/config.json
   40|       |  doctor                            read-only environment and guarantee report
   41|       |  status                            repository, dirty state, receipt, pending intents
   42|       |  disable | enable                  refuse / allow mutating commands
   43|       |
   44|       |PROPOSALS (multi-agent):
   45|       |  propose --agent A --task T1 --base <commit> --patch <file> [--key K]
   46|       |                                    admit an attributed, immutable proposal
   47|       |  proposals list                    list admitted proposals
   48|       |  proposals show <id>               one proposal
   49|       |  assemble --proposal <id>...       build an immutable candidate tree from proposals
   50|       |
   51|       |VERIFICATION AND PUBLICATION:
   52|       |  run [--candidate <id>] [--obligation ID]... [--timeout S]
   53|       |                                    verify the candidate tree, or the INDEX tree
   54|       |  publish [--candidate <id>] [--ref REF] [--expected-old OID] [--message M]
   55|       |          [--dry-run]               CAS-publish a verified tree
   56|       |  reconcile                         resolve pending intents by observation
   57|       |  explain [--tree OID | --candidate <id>]
   58|       |                                    receipt, proposals and journal for a tree
   59|       |
   60|       |  version                           tool, policy and schema versions
   61|       |  help                              this text
   62|       |
   63|       |Every command prints machine-readable JSON on stdout. `--json` is accepted for
   64|       |compatibility; JSON is already the default.
   65|       |Exit codes: 0 = PASSED/PUBLISHED/PLAN/INITIALIZED/APPROVED/ADMITTED/ASSEMBLED/
   66|       |RECORD/RECONCILED/EXPLAIN/STATUS/DISABLED/ENABLED, 1 = REFUSED/FAILED/UNKNOWN/
   67|       |UNSUPPORTED/NEEDS_RECONCILIATION/ERROR, 2 = usage.
   68|       |";
   69|       |

D:\App\Ravum-flow\crates\flow\src\proposal.rs:
    1|       |//! Proposals and exact-tree candidates.
    2|       |//!
    3|       |//! The contract, in the order the code enforces it:
    4|       |//!
    5|       |//! 1. A **proposal** is an immutable, attributed change set: `agent_id`,
    6|       |//!    `task_id`, `idempotency_key`, a pinned `base_commit`, and a Git patch whose
    7|       |//!    bytes are hashed into `content_digest`. Re-submitting the same key with the
    8|       |//!    same bytes returns the same proposal; the same key with different bytes is
    9|       |//!    refused (`IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`) — a correction is a new proposal id.
   10|       |//! 2. The patch is applied **only** to a temporary index (`GIT_INDEX_FILE`
   11|       |//!    pointing outside the repository). The user's worktree and index are never
   12|       |//!    read for the result and never written.
   13|       |//! 3. The resulting Git tree is the proposal's `tree_oid`. It is validated:
   14|       |//!    no absolute/traversal path, no write into `.git/` or `.flow/`, no gitlink,
   15|       |//!    no escaping symlink, no case-colliding pair, nothing the host cannot
   16|       |//!    represent faithfully.
   17|       |//! 4. A **candidate** is the immutable result of applying several proposals, in
   18|       |//!    the explicit order given, to their common base. The candidate id binds the
   19|       |//!    tree, the base and the ordered proposal list. Nothing touches a ref here.
   20|       |//! 5. Assembly refuses ambiguity instead of guessing: different bases, the same
   21|       |//!    path with incompatible operations (add/delete/rename/mode), or a hunk that
   22|       |//!    does not apply, produce `BASE_MISMATCH` / `PATH_OPERATION_CONFLICT` /
   23|       |//!    `CONFLICT` with the failing proposal named.
   24|       |
   25|       |use std::fs;
   26|       |use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
   27|       |
   28|       |use crate::gitcli::Git;
   29|       |use crate::json::{self, Json};
   30|       |use crate::paths;
   31|       |// One validator for every "is this a Git object id" question in the crate: a
   32|       |// second copy is a rule that can drift away from the trust boundary.
   33|       |use crate::paths::is_object_id;
   34|       |use crate::sha256::sha256_hex;
   35|       |use crate::state::{self, SCHEMA_CANDIDATE, SCHEMA_PROPOSAL};
   36|       |

D:\App\Ravum-flow\crates\flow\src\scratch.rs:
    1|       |//! Disposable verification directories ("scratch").
    2|       |//!
    3|       |//! [`materialise`] checks a commit's tree out into a **new, uniquely named
    4|       |//! directory outside the repository**, then removes the worktree's `.git` link
    5|       |//! so the candidate process cannot reach the repository's shared Git directory
    6|       |//! through ordinary repository discovery.
    7|       |//!
    8|       |//! # Guarantees (enforced here, with tests)
    9|       |//!
   10|       |//! * The directory is created with `create_dir`, so it did not exist before;
   11|       |//!   a name collision is retried, never reused or overwritten.
   12|       |//! * Nothing that already exists is deleted. `Drop` removes a directory only
   13|       |//!   when it still is a real directory (not a symlink/reparse point), still
   14|       |//!   resolves to the identity recorded at creation, and still carries the
   15|       |//!   ownership marker written after materialisation.
   16|       |//! * A base, or a resolved scratch path, inside the repository is refused.
   17|       |//! * Cleanup is bounded (wall clock and entry count) and never panics.
   18|       |//!
   19|       |//! # Non-guarantees (explicit, never called a sandbox)
   20|       |//!
   21|       |//! Removing `.git` only defeats *accidental or casual* repository discovery.
   22|       |//! A hostile candidate can still find repositories on the same machine (it is
   23|       |//! the same user, same filesystem), inspect its own environment, or detach
   24|       |//! from any kill. This module provides hygiene and bounded cleanup, **not**
   25|       |//! confinement; the crate has no sandbox and claims none.
   26|       |
   27|       |use std::fs;
   28|       |use std::io;
   29|       |use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
   30|       |use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering};
   31|       |use std::time::{Duration, Instant};
   32|       |
   33|       |use crate::gitcli::{self, Git, Obligation};
   34|       |
   35|       |/// Prefix of every scratch directory name, so leftovers are recognisable.

   37|       |
   38|       |/// Ownership marker written after `git worktree add` succeeded. `Drop` refuses

   41|       |

  283|       |/// Refuse to write through a symlink or a Windows reparse point (junction).
  284|       |///
  285|       |/// A repository can ship `.flow/journal.jsonl` or `.flow/receipts` as a symlink:
  286|       |/// cloning with `core.symlinks=true` (the default on Linux and macOS) then makes
  287|       |/// Flow append outside the workspace. Every write below `.flow/` therefore checks
  288|       |/// that each existing component is a real directory and that the target is not a
  289|       |/// link. This is a check-then-write, so it is not a defence against a *concurrent*

  451|       |/// Nanosecond-resolution suffix for journal intent ids.
  452|       |///
  453|       |/// The id used to be `second + commit prefix`. Two publications of the same
  454|       |/// tree to two refs within one second produce the same commit (same tree, same
  455|       |/// parent, same default message), hence the same id, and one of the two intents
  456|       |/// could then be closed by the other's outcome.
  457|     86|pub fn unique_suffix() -> u128 {

  461|     86|        .unwrap_or(0)

  559|       |/// Bind a receipt to its own content.
  560|       |///
  561|       |/// This detects accidental corruption and casual editing of a receipt on disk.
  562|       |/// It is **not** a signature and **not** protection against an attacker who can
  563|       |/// rewrite both the config and the receipt: that would require a key the Flow

  655|       |/// Civil date/time from the Unix epoch, UTC, without a dependency.
  656|    414|pub fn iso8601_now() -> String {
  657|    414|    let secs = SystemTime::now()
  658|    414|        .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
  659|    414|        .map(|d| d.as_secs())
  660|    414|        .unwrap_or(0);

  662|    414|    let rem = secs % 86_400;
  663|    414|    let (year, month, day) = civil_from_days(days);
  664|    414|    format!(
  665|       |        "{:04}-{:02}-{:02}T{:02}:{:02}:{:02}Z",
  666|       |        year,
  667|       |        month,
  668|       |        day,
  669|    414|        rem / 3600,
  670|    414|        (rem % 3600) / 60,
  671|    414|        rem % 60
  672|       |    )
  673|    414|}
  674|       |
  675|    416|fn civil_from_days(days: i64) -> (i64, u32, u32) {
  676|       |    // Howard Hinnant's algorithm, adapted from days since 1970-01-01.
  677|    416|    let z = days + 719_468;
  678|    416|    let era = if z >= 0 { z } else { z - 146_096 } / 146_097;
                                                   ^0
  ------------------
  |  Branch (678:18): [True: 2, False: 0]
  ------------------
  |  Branch (678:18): [True: 414, False: 0]
  ------------------
  679|    416|    let doe = (z - era * 146_097) as u64;
  680|    416|    let yoe = (doe - doe / 1460 + doe / 36_524 - doe / 146_096) / 365;
  681|    416|    let y = yoe as i64 + era * 400;
  682|    416|    let doy = doe - (365 * yoe + yoe / 4 - yoe / 100);
  683|    416|    let mp = (5 * doy + 2) / 153;
  684|    416|    let d = (doy - (153 * mp + 2) / 5 + 1) as u32;
  685|    416|    let m = if mp < 10 { mp + 3 } else { mp - 9 } as u32;
                                       ^415            ^1
  ------------------
  |  Branch (685:16): [True: 1, False: 1]
  ------------------
  |  Branch (685:16): [True: 414, False: 0]
  ------------------
  686|    416|    (if m <= 2 { y + 1 } else { y }, m, d)
                               ^1             ^415
  ------------------
  |  Branch (686:9): [True: 1, False: 1]
  ------------------
  |  Branch (686:9): [True: 0, False: 414]
